```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0006
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 146d-146e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 146d-146e
  start_marker: 146d
  end_marker: 146e
  start_char: 8622
  end_char: 9387
  text_sha256: 8127f93636b5df89cc2032c4c3ee4a0bbd6bf574782df09f20c54147e47d289c
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: When asked what knowledge is, answering by enumerating kinds of knowledge (what knowledge is of, or how many kinds there are) misses the question; the question aims at grasping knowledge itself, not at counting its instances.
greek_terms:
  - αὐτὸ ὅτι ποτ’ ἐστίν
  - τίνων
  - ὁπόσαι
  - ἀριθμῆσαι
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 146e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 146e
      start_marker: 146e
      end_marker: 146e
      start_char: 9000
      end_char: 9387
      text_sha256: 2285d62f6d120b9d55c25ff65d8070757d545ab36d320fbe48ee3ee635ebba39
    speaker: Socrates
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0422
limits: This records a methodological rule stated by Socrates about how definitional questions should be answered; it does not establish that Theaetetus' enumeration is false, only that it fails to answer the question as posed.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

content: When asked what something is (e.g., clay), answering by listing different kinds of clay (potters' clay, oven-makers' clay, brick-makers' clay) is ridiculous; the questioner expects to understand the thing itself from the answer.
greek_terms:
  - πηλός
  - πηλὸς ὁ τῶν χυτρέων
  - πηλὸς ὁ τῶν ἰπνοπλαθῶν
  - πηλὸς ὁ τῶν πλινθουργῶν
```


```yaml

  - προμήκη
  - προμήκη ἀριθμὸν

  - kind: reported

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0011
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΘΕΑΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Lines that square the equilateral and plane number were defined as 'lengths,' while lines that square the oblong number were defined as 'powers,' being incommensurable in length with the former but commensurable in the plane figures they produce.
greek_terms:
  - μῆκος
  - δυνάμεις
  - οὐ συμμέτρους
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 148a-148b

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0427
limits: This is a technical geometry report, not an epistemic thesis; it serves as a paradigm for the kind of collection under one eidos that Socrates asks for.
review_status: accepted
```








```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0047
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 156e-157b

claim_kind: thesis

  []

review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0048

```



```yaml

final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0461
limits: Socrates poses this as an apparent refutation of the Protagorean-flux doctrine that perception is knowledge and that what appears to each person is for that person. He does not assert it as his own settled view; he presents it as a remaining difficulty that must be addressed.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0053
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 158b-158c

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: It is difficult to provide a proof to distinguish whether at any present moment we are asleep and dreaming all that we think, or awake and conversing with one another in reality.
greek_terms:
  - τεκμήριον
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 158b-158c

final_status: posed_only

limits: Socrates poses this as a difficulty for anyone who would appeal to the dream/waking distinction to refute the Protagorean thesis. He does not assert that no such proof exists, only that it is difficult to supply one. Theaetetus immediately agrees at 158c but this record is limited to Socrates' posing of the problem.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0054

speaker: ΘΕΑΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is indeed puzzling by what proof one could show whether we are awake or dreaming, because all the same features accompany both states in parallel — nothing prevents our seeming to converse even in sleep, and when we seem to recount dreams within a dream the resemblance is strange.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 158c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: Theaetetus affirms the difficulty posed by Socrates and elaborates the parallel-structure problem. The record does not commit to the claim that waking and dreaming are indistinguishable in principle, only that this is a genuine puzzle.
review_status: rejected
```


```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: What we perceive by seeing or hearing — do we also simultaneously know it? For example, before learning the speech of foreigners, do we not hear them when they speak, or do we hear and also know what they say? And again, not knowing letters, when looking at them, do we say we do not see them, or that we know them if we do see them?
greek_terms:
  - ὁρᾶν
  - ἀκούειν
  - αἰσθανόμεθα
  - ἐπίστασθαι
  - βαρβάρων
  - γράμματα

final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0473
limits: These are questions posed for examination, not claims Socrates endorses. The examples test the identity of perception and knowledge, not define either term.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0083
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 163e-164b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 163e-164b

  end_char: 45836
  text_sha256: e82f0997e19399fa3d803a395052defe18f0682352ccda3e71f8e4b58954f332
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The premise that not seeing is not knowing follows if seeing is knowing. On this basis, one who has seen and become knowledgeable, having closed his eyes, remembers but does not see; therefore he does not know that of which he became knowledgeable while still remembering it — which was agreed to be a monstrosity. So something impossible follows if one says knowledge and perception are the same.
greek_terms:
  - μύσῃ
  - μέμνηται
  - οὐχ ὁρᾷ
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 163e-164b

limits: This records the logical structure of the challenge to the identity thesis. It does not assert that Socrates endorses the conclusion.
review_status: accepted
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0090

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: In dialectical discourse one should keep agonistic sparring separate from serious conversation; in serious conversation one should correct the interlocutor by pointing out only those errors that the interlocutor himself, as a result of his own thinking and prior associations, has fallen into.

  []

review_status: accepted
```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0098

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: report
content: According to Protagoras's logos, whenever someone forms a judgment and declares an opinion, that opinion is true for that person, but others either cannot be judges of that judgment or always judge it true — yet countless people oppose it, holding that one judges and opines falsely.

  - ἄνθρωπον

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0099
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 170e-171a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 170e-171a
  start_marker: 170e
  end_marker: 171a
  start_char: 59269
  end_char: 60115
  text_sha256: 8547d1e1d3117efb41a36739295a9a96748499360587bce4fbfa990670a3d025
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If neither Protagoras himself nor the many believed that man is the measure — just as they do not — then the truth that he wrote would be truth for no one; and if he believed it but the multitude did not share the belief, then in proportion as those to whom it does not seem are more numerous than those to whom it seems, to that extent it is not rather than it is.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 170e-171a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 170e-171a
      start_marker: 170e
      end_marker: 171a
      start_char: 59269
      end_char: 60115
      text_sha256: 8547d1e1d3117efb41a36739295a9a96748499360587bce4fbfa990670a3d025
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0492
limits: This is a self-refutation argument; if the measure doctrine is itself a matter of seeming, its truth depends on how many it seems true to. The record does not establish that Socrates endorses this as a general epistemic principle independently of the dialectical context.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  text_sha256: 1dc2ced1ecc757000392bf073f994747eb1b9b089240afca2ba71d489f44b394
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: From all sides the measure doctrine will be disputed — or rather conceded by Protagoras — so that he himself will agree that neither a dog nor any chance man is the measure of anything he has not learned.
greek_terms:
  - μέτρον
  - κύνα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 171b-171c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 171b-171c

      end_char: 60969
      text_sha256: 1dc2ced1ecc757000392bf073f994747eb1b9b089240afca2ba71d489f44b394
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0496
limits: This is the conclusion Socrates draws from the self-refutation argument. The record does not establish that Protagoras would in fact concede this, only that the logic of his position forces it.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0104
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Since the measure doctrine is disputed by all, Protagoras's Truth would be true for no one, neither for anyone else nor for Protagoras himself.

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 171c

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0105

review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0107
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 171d-171e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 171d-171e
  start_marker: 171d
  end_marker: 171e
  start_char: 60969
  end_char: 61844
  text_sha256: 083f12a11044c582beee9c045a2c7cb901719697fe0cab61c0270b3cbc4371d8
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The argument would most stand firm on the line we earlier sketched in defence of Protagoras: that for the most part things are for each as they seem — hot, dry, sweet, and all things of that type; but if anywhere he will concede that one person differs from another, it would be concerning healthy and sick matters, where he would be willing to say that not every woman, child, or beast is competent to cure itself by knowing what is healthy for itself, but here indeed one differs from another."
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 171d-171e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 171d-171e
      start_marker: 171d
      end_marker: 171e
      start_char: 60969
      end_char: 61844
      text_sha256: 083f12a11044c582beee9c045a2c7cb901719697fe0cab61c0270b3cbc4371d8
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0500
limits: This is Socrates' proposal of a qualified version of the measure doctrine where perceptual qualities are relative but health matters admit expertise. The record does not establish that Protagoras would accept this qualification or that Socrates endorses it as his own final view.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0108
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 172a-172b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 172a-172b
  start_marker: 172a
  end_marker: 172b
  start_char: 61844
  end_char: 62756
  text_sha256: 1aaffcf10e08cefd5231944021c97c943b4ac7211d645f12a4e6dc9af53271f5
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: In matters of justice and injustice, holy and unholy, whatever a city lays down as lawful by its own judgment is true for that city, and no individual or city is wiser than another in these matters.
greek_terms:
  - καλὰ
  - αἰσχρὰ
  - δίκαια
  - ἄδικα
  - ὅσια
  - μή
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 172a-172b
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 172a-172b
      start_marker: 172a
      end_marker: 172b
      start_char: 61844
      end_char: 62756
      text_sha256: 1aaffcf10e08cefd5231944021c97c943b4ac7211d645f12a4e6dc9af53271f5
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0501
limits: Socrates does not endorse this Protagorean thesis himself; he reports it as a view Protagoras' followers or those who do not entirely reject his account would hold. This record does not attribute the thesis to Socrates.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0109
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 172a-172b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 172a-172b
  start_marker: 172a
  end_marker: 172b

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Concerning what is advantageous or disadvantageous for a city to lay down, one adviser differs from another and one city's opinion differs from another's with respect to truth.

  - συμφέροντα
  - σύμβουλόν

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0110
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 172b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: report
content: Protagoreans maintain that nothing is just or holy by nature with an essence of its own; rather, whatever is collectively deemed so becomes true at that time and for as long as it is held.

  - φύσει
  - οὐσίαν
  - κοινῇ δόξαν

review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0112
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 172d-172e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 172d-172e
  start_marker: 172d
  end_marker: 172e
  start_char: 63147
  end_char: 63970
  text_sha256: 186f250353faf75925883a4eb2a2aa36b7975a0cf1b17f3881e495e10a61cd40
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Philosophers enjoy leisure and make their arguments in peace at their own pace, caring only whether they hit upon what is, and taking no concern over speaking at length or briefly.
greek_terms:
  - σχολή
  - εἰρήνῃ
  - ὄντος
  - μακρῶν
  - βραχέων
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 172d-172e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0505
limits: This thesis describes the condition of philosophical discourse by contrast with forensic speech. It does not claim that all philosophers succeed in hitting upon what is.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0113
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 172e-173a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 172e-173a
  start_marker: 172e
  end_marker: 173a
  start_char: 63561
  end_char: 64426
  text_sha256: d43ac5d4dad014d9dc594c445268d88e4d8cfa9bc9ba65b45ff1e05ab479bd21
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Forensic speakers are always in a hurry, constrained by the water-clock, compelled by an opposing litigant, cannot speak about whatever they wish, and must keep within a written indictment; their contests always concern a fellow-slave before a seated master holding a case, often running for their life.
greek_terms:
  - ὕδωρ
  - ἀντίδικος
  - ἀντωμοσίαν
  - ὁμοδούλου
  - δεσπότην
  - ψυχῆς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 172e-173a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 172e-173a
      start_marker: 172e
      end_marker: 173a
      start_char: 63561
      end_char: 64426
      text_sha256: d43ac5d4dad014d9dc594c445268d88e4d8cfa9bc9ba65b45ff1e05ab479bd21
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0506
limits: This describes the conditions of forensic oratory as Socrates sees them; it does not establish that no forensic speaker can speak truthfully under these constraints.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0114
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 173a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 173a
  start_marker: 173a
  end_marker: 173a
  start_char: 63970
  end_char: 64426
  text_sha256: e523513ab464c6a3d98c15a4980fa2e44ddb1e3b31ee8b8dde72796b2c7a79a7
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: As a result of these conditions, forensic speakers become tense and sharp, knowing how to flatter the master in speech and get around him in deed, and become small and crooked in their souls.
greek_terms:
  - ἔντονοι
  - δριμεῖς
  - θωπεῦσαι
  - σμικροὶ
  - οὐκ ὀρθοὶ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 173a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 173a
      start_marker: 173a
      end_marker: 173a
      start_char: 63970
      end_char: 64426
      text_sha256: e523513ab464c6a3d98c15a4980fa2e44ddb1e3b31ee8b8dde72796b2c7a79a7
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0507
limits: This is an asserted causal thesis about the deformative effects of forensic practice on the soul. It does not claim that every individual forensic speaker exhibits these traits.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0115
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 173a-173b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 173a-173b
  start_marker: 173a
  end_marker: 173b
  start_char: 63970
  end_char: 64853
  text_sha256: 8ce9aa4822cb362f40e9ffb4dbb61519eb2cae8b3481f33cc2691a41c3e05948
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The enslavement from youth strips forensic speakers of growth, straightness, and liberality, forcing crooked actions, imposing great fears on tender souls; unable to endure with justice and truth, they turn to falsehood and mutual wrongdoing and are bent and broken, ending up as adults with nothing healthy in their thought.
greek_terms:
  - αὔξην
  - εὐθύ
  - ἐλευθέριον
  - δουλεία
  - σκολιά
  - ψεῦδός
  - ὑγιὲς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 173a-173b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 173a-173b
      start_marker: 173a
      end_marker: 173b
      start_char: 63970
      end_char: 64853
      text_sha256: 8ce9aa4822cb362f40e9ffb4dbb61519eb2cae8b3481f33cc2691a41c3e05948
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0508
limits: This is a causal-psychological thesis about the deformative effects of forensic training from youth. It does not assert that no forensic speaker can recover or that the philosopher is immune to analogous deformation.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0116
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 173c-173d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The leading philosophers from youth do not know the way to the agora or where the law court or council chamber or any other public meeting-place of the city is.

  - ἀγορὰν
  - δικαστήριον
  - βουλευτήριον
  - συνέδριον

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0117
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 173d-173e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 173d-173e
  start_marker: 173d
  end_marker: 173e

  end_char: 66102
  text_sha256: 6d5edb70fe20a4fc7794b515aabe33b9e3223968568eec3c04bc1475d38f49a4
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The philosopher's thought, regarding all these civic matters as small and nothing, flies everywhere according to Pindar — beneath the earth, measuring its surfaces, and above the heavens, studying the stars — and investigates every nature of each whole being, never letting itself down to what lies near at hand.
greek_terms:
  - διάνοια
  - πέτεται
  - τᾶς τε γᾶς ὑπένερθε
  - οὐρανοῦ θ’ ὕπερ
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 173d-173e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 173d-173e
      start_marker: 173d
      end_marker: 173e
      start_char: 65293
      end_char: 66102
      text_sha256: 6d5edb70fe20a4fc7794b515aabe33b9e3223968568eec3c04bc1475d38f49a4
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: This is a description of the philosopher's intellectual orientation, not a method rule. It does not claim that philosophers are incapable of attending to practical matters, only that their thought is directed elsewhere.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0118
source_work: Theaetetus

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0119
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: What a human being is, and what it befits such a nature to do or undergo that differs from other creatures — this is what the philosopher investigates and takes trouble to examine.

  []

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 174b
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0120
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 174e-175b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 174e-175b

  end_char: 69040
  text_sha256: 1465ae1173e6b40a5d7b500d43cc9977c06b88c544b7e52247c636e68f8a6e47
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Those who hymn their lineage and boast of noble ancestry are fixated on what is narrow and small, because through lack of education they cannot look toward the whole or reckon that every person has had countless ancestors of every station.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 174e-175b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0121
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 175c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

  end_char: 69509
  text_sha256: 2bdaae4c572b7021660b9a560546a36ed232a3fb25560b14988cbbe478ab2437
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: When the philosopher draws someone upward and he is willing to step out of the question "what wrong do I do to you or you to me?" into an examination of justice itself and injustice itself — what each is and how they differ — the inquiry moves from particular grievances to what justice and injustice are in themselves.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 175c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 175c

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: This is a description of philosophical method, not a definition of justice. It does not specify the result of such an examination, only the transition from the particular to the general.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0122
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 175e-176a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 175e-176a
  start_marker: 175e
  end_marker: 176a
  start_char: 69895
  end_char: 70696
  text_sha256: f7576f07f2100934001d2c37f1f31ca09cac49b39c649ec98ecf33d0fb0cdaab
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There are two contrasting types: the one truly reared in freedom and leisure, whom you call the philosopher, and the other capable of performing all servile tasks sharply and keenly but unable to arrange his cloak like a free man or grasp the harmony of speech to hymn correctly the life of gods and blessed men."
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 175e-176a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 175e-176a
      start_marker: 175e
      end_marker: 176a
      start_char: 69895
      end_char: 70696
      text_sha256: f7576f07f2100934001d2c37f1f31ca09cac49b39c649ec98ecf33d0fb0cdaab
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0513
limits: This is a contrast of character types, not a definition of knowledge. It does not claim that all non-philosophers are servile, only that the two types differ in what they are trained to do.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0123
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 176a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

  start_marker: 176a

  end_char: 70696
  text_sha256: 90059130e823808333c5f1823f429bb11141115d4ceaf1481fb02fee9559c0bb
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is not possible for evils to be destroyed, for there must always be something opposite to the good; nor can evils be established among the gods, but they must haunt mortal nature and this region by necessity.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 176a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0124
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 176a-176b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: One should try to flee from here to there as quickly as possible, and this flight is becoming like god insofar as possible; becoming like god consists in becoming just and holy with wisdom.
greek_terms:
  - φυγὴ δὲ ὁμοίωσις θεῷ κατὰ τὸ δυνατόν
  - δίκαιον καὶ ὅσιον μετὰ φρονήσεως

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0125
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 176b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is not easy to persuade people that one should pursue virtue and flee vice not for the sake of appearing good and avoiding a bad reputation, but for the truth of the matter; the common saying that one should pursue virtue to avoid seeming bad is an old wives' tale.
greek_terms:
  - γραῶν ὕθλος
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 176b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: This is a thesis about the proper motive for virtue, not a definition of knowledge. It does not specify what the truth of the matter is beyond the assimilation thesis in 176a-b.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0126
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Knowledge of how to become as just as possible constitutes true wisdom and virtue, while ignorance of this is manifest foolishness and vice, and other supposed clevernesses and wisdoms — those in political powers and those in arts — are vulgar and banausic.

  - γνῶσις
  - σοφία καὶ ἀρετὴ ἀληθινή
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0127
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0128
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 176e-177a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 176e-177a
  start_marker: 176e
  end_marker: 177a
  start_char: 71997
  end_char: 72694
  text_sha256: 92facc0d4389151872de3fd1a3ad0caf2a9fe6cb2bef6c3fa456da24e4ee2db8
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There are two standing paradigms in reality: one divine and most happy, the other godless and most wretched; unjust men, through folly, unwittingly assimilate themselves to the wretched paradigm by their unjust actions and are made unlike the divine one, and they pay the penalty by living a life resembling that to which they are assimilated."
greek_terms:
  - παραδειγμάτων
  - τοῦ μὲν θείου εὐδαιμονεστάτου
  - τοῦ δὲ ἀθέου ἀθλιωτάτου
  - ὁμοιούμενοι
  - ἀνομοιούμενοι
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 176e-177a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 176e-177a
      start_marker: 176e
      end_marker: 177a
      start_char: 71997
      end_char: 72694
      text_sha256: 92facc0d4389151872de3fd1a3ad0caf2a9fe6cb2bef6c3fa456da24e4ee2db8
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0519
limits: This is asserted as a statement about cosmic justice, not derived from the definition of knowledge under examination. The claim is presented within a framework of paradigms and assimilation, not as a dialectically tested thesis.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0129
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 177a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 177a
  start_marker: 177a
  end_marker: 177a
  start_char: 72236
  end_char: 72694
  text_sha256: 8cdbb22f754d32aeb0ebdcb64d0766c6e2a672cf2234f104ff356861c85bfbc3
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: After death, the place pure of evils will not receive those who fail to rid themselves of their cleverness, and they will forever keep here a mode of life similar to themselves, bad men consorting with bad men.
greek_terms:
  - δεινότητος
  - ὁ τῶν κακῶν καθαρὸς τόπος
  - κακοὶ κακοῖς συνόντες
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 177a

limits: This extends the paradigm thesis into an eschatological claim. It is presented as what would be said and dismissed as foolish by the clever, not as a dialectically established conclusion.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0130
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 177b-177c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: When those who hold such views are compelled to give and receive an account in private and endure the discussion manfully for a long time rather than flee, their rhetoric withers away and they end up displeasing themselves about what they say, appearing no different from children.
greek_terms:
  - ἡ ῥητορικὴ ἐκείνη πως ἀπομαραίνεται
  - παίδων μηδὲν δοκεῖν διαφέρειν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 177b-177c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

      end_marker: 177c

      end_char: 73583

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0521
limits: This is an empirical claim about the effect of sustained dialectical examination on those who rely on rhetorical cleverness. It is asserted but not demonstrated within the dialogue; it serves as a transition back to the main argument.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0131
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 177c-177d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 177c-177d
  start_marker: 177c
  end_marker: 177d
  start_char: 73132
  end_char: 73948
  text_sha256: 8e1a60ed6d7c06461375c48cd7fb949c0110d4386fbc17dde7e3617205bb560e
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: report
content: Proponents of the doctrine that being is in flux and that what seems to each person is for that person are willing to maintain this in most matters, and especially concerning just things — that whatever a city lays down as seeming just to itself, these things are just for the city that laid them down as long as they remain in force — but concerning good things no one is brave enough to contend that whatever a city thinks beneficial and lays down for itself is beneficial for as long as it remains in force, unless someone merely speaks about the name.
greek_terms:
  - τὴν φερομένην οὐσίαν
  - τὸ ἀεὶ δοκοῦν ἑκάστῳ τοῦτο καὶ εἶναι τούτῳ ᾧ δοκεῖ
  - τὰ δίκαια
  - τἀγαθὰ
  - ὠφέλιμα
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 177c-177d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 177c-177d
      start_marker: 177c
      end_marker: 177d
      start_char: 73132
      end_char: 73948
      text_sha256: 8e1a60ed6d7c06461375c48cd7fb949c0110d4386fbc17dde7e3617205bb560e
  - kind: challenged
    stephanus_span: 177c-177d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 177c-177d
      start_marker: 177c
      end_marker: 177d
      start_char: 73132
      end_char: 73948
      text_sha256: 8e1a60ed6d7c06461375c48cd7fb949c0110d4386fbc17dde7e3617205bb560e
final_status: unresolved_challenge
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0522
limits: The report covers the flux/measure doctrine's scope limitation regarding the good. The challenge is that the doctrine's proponents will not maintain it for the beneficial/good, only for the name. This is a reported asymmetry, not yet refuted.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0132
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 177e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 177e
  start_marker: 177e
  end_marker: 177e
  start_char: 73948
  end_char: 74232
  text_sha256: acc861b79d7372a2dbf56d7f8f57c46cd5dd7207635c2d65fd3a32e02c0b3fa5
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: When a city legislates, it aims at the thing named, not merely the name, and lays down all laws, insofar as it thinks and is able, as most beneficial for itself.
greek_terms:
  - τὸ πρᾶγμα τὸ ὀνομαζόμενον
  - ὠφελιμωτάτους
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 177e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 177e
      start_marker: 177e
      end_marker: 177e

      end_char: 74232
      text_sha256: acc861b79d7372a2dbf56d7f8f57c46cd5dd7207635c2d65fd3a32e02c0b3fa5
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0523
limits: This is asserted as a premise about the aim of legislation. It does not yet establish whether cities always succeed at this aim. The claim is part of the developing argument against the Protagorean position.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0133
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 178a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 178a
  start_marker: 178a

speaker: ΘΕΟ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: A city does not always hit upon what is beneficial in its legislation, but each city often errs.
greek_terms:
  - διαμαρτάνει
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 178a
    source_ref:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0134
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 178a-178b

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis

  - τὸν μέλλοντα χρόνον
  - εἰς τὸν ἔπειτα χρόνον
stance_events:

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0135
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 178b-178c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 178b-178c
  start_marker: 178b
  end_marker: 178c
  start_char: 74660
  end_char: 75500
  text_sha256: d5c202a534d7269accaa20e0f692493327e6897f52c5a2f24b993740416315d2
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: report
content: Protagoras's measure doctrine holds that a human being has the criterion within himself for present perceptual qualities such as white, heavy, and light, and whatever he experiences and thereby believes, these beliefs are true and real for him.
greek_terms:
  - πάντων μέτρον ἄνθρωπός ἐστιν
  - τὸ κριτήριον
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0136
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 178c-178d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 178c-178d
  start_marker: 178c
  end_marker: 178d
  start_char: 75048
  end_char: 75923
  text_sha256: f9c4bfa9c4f1a502b3f3bcabb48ef6fc97ffaf1033022e6475e623f809aac432
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The Protagorean measure doctrine does not extend to judgments about the future: one does not have the criterion of future events within oneself, and what one believes will happen does not thereby come to be for the believer; expert judgment about future states — for example, a doctor's judgment about future fever, a farmer's about future sweetness of wine, a physical trainer's about future harmonious condition, and a cook's about future pleasure of a feast — is authoritative over the layman's."
greek_terms:
  - τὸ κριτήριον
  - γελοῖον μεντἂν εἴη
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 178c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 178c
      start_marker: 178c
      end_marker: 178c
      start_char: 75048
      end_char: 75500
      text_sha256: 00bb491c8322bfa6ecec0a90945d4bb30bcd3ee90b2bcff55d894bfe0de1a4f6
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 178c-178d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 178c-178d
      start_marker: 178c
      end_marker: 178d
      start_char: 75048
      end_char: 75923
      text_sha256: f9c4bfa9c4f1a502b3f3bcabb48ef6fc97ffaf1033022e6475e623f809aac432
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0527
limits: This is posed as a challenge to the Protagorean position by testing whether the measure doctrine applies to future judgments. The series of counterexamples (doctor, farmer, trainer, cook) is meant to show the absurdity of extending the doctrine to the future, but the argument is still being developed and has not yet been fully conceded or refuted by a Protagorean interlocutor.
review_status: accepted
```






```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0146

claim_kind: report

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0147
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0149
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 181e-182a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 181e-182a
  start_marker: 181e
  end_marker: 182a
  start_char: 82183
  end_char: 83052
  text_sha256: 6961f166ed0e7388ef2d164bf281a8b2a5a8f139ff9bd60f9c35815f3d57baf6
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If the all-is-in-motion thesis is true, then everything must always undergo both kinds of motion (locomotion and alteration), since if anything underwent only one kind, there would be both moving and standing things, and it would be no more correct to say all things move than that all things stand.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 181e-182a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 181e-182a
      start_marker: 181e
      end_marker: 182a
      start_char: 82183
      end_char: 83052
      text_sha256: 6961f166ed0e7388ef2d164bf281a8b2a5a8f139ff9bd60f9c35815f3d57baf6
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0538
limits: This record does not establish that the Heracliteans actually held that everything undergoes both kinds of motion; Theodorus only conjectures they would say so.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0150
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 182a-182b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 182a-182b

  - ποιόν
  - ποιότητα

  - obs_theaetetus_0539
limits: This record restates only what Socrates says the universal-motion theorists maintain; it does not evaluate whether the report is accurate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0151
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 182c-182d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 182c-182d
  start_marker: 182c
  end_marker: 182d
  start_char: 83435
  end_char: 84279
  text_sha256: a8dad0128567461f53f1bee30b50eda6d9eb4061a66a3bbee3e101622501e7de
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If everything undergoes both locomotion and alteration, then even the quality of whiteness does not remain stable in what flows but itself flows and changes into another colour, so that one can never correctly name any colour.
greek_terms:
  - λευκότητος
  - χρόαν
  - ῥεῖν
  - ῥοήν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 182c-182d

final_status: left_standing

  - obs_theaetetus_0540
limits: This record does not establish that the universal-motion theorists themselves accepted this consequence; it is Socrates' argument that the position entails an inability to fix any predicate.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0152
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 182d-182e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 182d-182e
  start_marker: 182d
  end_marker: 182e
  start_char: 83869
  end_char: 84692
  text_sha256: dce48554338e3dc8fcb5846246a68b4123959d6b6fedc98c3059b0a3081f61f1
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If everything moves, no perception such as seeing or hearing remains stable as seeing or hearing, so one should no more call something seeing than not-seeing, nor any other perception more than its negation.
greek_terms:
  - ὁρᾶν
  - ἀκούειν
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0153
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 182e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 182e
  start_marker: 182e
  end_marker: 182e
  start_char: 84279
  end_char: 84692
  text_sha256: 198bb0f41b956cd90766715820bf98dafa8dcf73d228cf6e4936f6b5b6e2baeb
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Since perception is knowledge (as agreed by Socrates and Theaetetus) and perception turns out to be no more perception than not-perception under universal motion, the answer 'knowledge is perception' is no more knowledge than not-knowledge.
greek_terms:
  - ἐπιστήμη
  - ἐπιστήμην
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 182e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 182e
      start_marker: 182e
      end_marker: 182e
      start_char: 84279
      end_char: 84692
      text_sha256: 198bb0f41b956cd90766715820bf98dafa8dcf73d228cf6e4936f6b5b6e2baeb
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0542
limits: This record does not claim that the definition 'knowledge is perception' is independently refuted; the refutation is conditional on the universal-motion hypothesis. It also does not establish that Theaetetus or Theodorus concede the refutation of the original definition at this point.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0154
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 183a-183b

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 183a-183b

  end_marker: 183b

  end_char: 85671
  text_sha256: aa4087cba769d814c2bec59a5c8789abd116c45725a64047e526aee966fa45ea
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If everything moves, every answer, about whatever one answers, is equally correct whether expressed with 'it is thus' or 'it is not thus' — or rather one must not use 'thus' or 'not thus' at all, since even those words imply stability; only an indefinite phrase like 'not even thus' could fit.
greek_terms:
  - οὕτω
  - μὴ οὕτω
  - ἄπειρον
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 183a-183b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 183a-183b
      start_marker: 183a
      end_marker: 183b
      start_char: 84692
      end_char: 85671
      text_sha256: aa4087cba769d814c2bec59a5c8789abd116c45725a64047e526aee966fa45ea
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0543
limits: This record does not establish that the universal-motion theorists themselves adopted 'not even thus' as their preferred expression; it is Socrates' conclusion about what their hypothesis entails for discourse.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  text_sha256: ea004192586b20698fc76fcb77101c491e8a494fc51a820810df5c65fe8af028
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Sense-perceptions (pathemata) that reach the soul through the body are present by nature to humans and beasts straight from birth, but the calculations (analogismata) concerning these toward being and benefit come only with difficulty, in time, through much practice and education, and only to those to whom they come at all.
greek_terms:
  - παθήματα
  - ἀναλογίσματα
  - οὐσίαν
  - ὠφέλειαν
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 186b-186c

      stephanus_span: 186b-186c

      end_marker: 186c
      start_char: 91545
      end_char: 92364
      text_sha256: ea004192586b20698fc76fcb77101c491e8a494fc51a820810df5c65fe8af028
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0554
limits: Socrates poses this as a question; Theaetetus assents at 186c but the final status reflects the last stance event recorded here. The claim does not specify which beings are capable of such calculations nor the precise nature of the education required.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0172
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 186d
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0173

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0174
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 187a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 187a

  text_sha256: f57f03d1835920f7da85e866008aaf8bac138544a61f97564f3f8b9e445b267b
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: The inquiry began not to find what knowledge is not, but to find what it is; nevertheless enough progress has been made to stop seeking knowledge in sense-perception and to seek it instead in whatever the soul has when it busies itself by itself concerning the things that are.
greek_terms:
  - ἐπιστήμη
  - αἰσθήσει
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 187a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0557
limits: Socrates states this as a procedural orientation, not as a definition of knowledge. The record does not claim that this rules out all possible roles for perception in knowledge.
review_status: accepted
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0177

He leaves open that if this definition does not hold up under examination, they will try another. The record does not assess whether the definition survives subsequent examination.
review_status: rejected
```




```yaml

      end_char: 100161
      text_sha256: 00dc18d0746db1cb783108bf53386234014b6b5e1ad7ea9fa4c2bcd142d256d7
final_status: unresolved_challenge
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0565
limits: "Challenge at 190a-190b is implicit: if thinking is silent speech and false belief is other-believing, false belief would require saying to oneself that one thing is another (e.g., the beautiful is ugly), which Theaetetus denies ever doing. The definition is not yet refuted, only called into question."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0191
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 190d-190e

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Allodoxia is impossible both when someone judges both things and when someone judges only one, so if anyone defines false judgment as allodoxia, he says nothing.
greek_terms:
  - ἀλλοδοξεῖν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 190d-190e
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:

limits: The thesis only rules out defining false judgment as allodoxia; it does not rule out other possible accounts of false judgment. The refutation is conditional on the earlier agreement that judging is a saying to oneself.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0193
source_work: theaetetus
stephanus_span: 191c-191d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 191c-191d
  start_marker: 191c
  end_marker: 191d
  start_char: 102270
  end_char: 103149
  text_sha256: 1530f2ffb549bb05fff3af89ca07ff33a972954d29de0375bb39888b44072132
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: There is a wax block in our souls, a gift of Memory, mother of the Muses, in which imprints from perceptions and thoughts are stamped like seal-rings; whatever is imprinted is remembered and known as long as the image remains, and whatever is wiped out or cannot be imprinted is forgotten and not known.
greek_terms:
  - κήρινον ἐκμαγεῖον
  - Μνημοσύνης
  - ἐνσημαινομένους
  - σημεῖα
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 191c-191d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 191c-191d
      start_marker: 191c
      end_marker: 191d
      start_char: 102270
      end_char: 103149
      text_sha256: 1530f2ffb549bb05fff3af89ca07ff33a972954d29de0375bb39888b44072132
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0573
limits: The wax block is introduced as a model for examination, not as a doctrine asserted as true. It is a hypothetical device for testing an account of false judgment.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0194

  []

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0195
source_work: theaetetus

  text_sha256: 9449c37299493a188ea9a65f64cec397d519dc0eb3164b0209518299aa2ac2b9
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Under the wax-block model, it is impossible for someone who knows something and has its memorial imprint but does not perceive it to judge it to be something else he knows, having that imprint too but not perceiving it; and similarly impossible across many combinations of what is known or perceived and what is not known or perceived.
greek_terms:
  - μνημεῖον
  - τύπον
  - σφραγῖδα
  - σημεῖον
stance_events:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: Socrates enumerates many impossible combinations for false judgment under the wax-block model but leaves open only those where the two items mismatch in the know and perceive dimension. The impossibility claims assume the wax-block model introduced hypothetically at 191c-d.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
content: Differences in the quality of the wax in the soul — deep, plentiful, smooth, and well-kneaded versus shaggy, dirty, wet, or hard — explain why some people are quick to learn, have good memory, and form true opinions, while others do not.

  []
stance_events:

    stephanus_span: 194c-194d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []

review_status: rejected
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0211
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The case where someone thinks that the twelve in the wax block are eleven returns us to the earlier difficulty: the person mistakes one thing he knows for another thing he knows, which was earlier judged impossible."
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 196b-196c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: Socrates argues that the eleven-twelve error about pure numbers replicates the earlier paradox of knowing something but mistaking it for another known thing. The record does not establish that this paradox is in fact unresolvable; it marks the breakdown of the coupling account.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0212

review_status: rejected
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0214

speaker: ΣΩ.

  - ἐπιστήμη

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0215

claim_kind: thesis

  - ἐπιστάμεθα

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
  text_sha256: bae08560b7a4aa8bfeb6777c97345d46b74c6cbf505c94cd2617a8d9c05fc3d0
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: It is possible to possess knowledge without having it, just as one who keeps wild birds in an aviary possesses them and has the power to take hold of them whenever he wishes, but does not have them in hand at every moment.
greek_terms:
  - περιστερεῶνα
  - κέκτηται
  - ἔχειν
  - δύναμιν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 197c-197d

      end_char: 115740
      text_sha256: bae08560b7a4aa8bfeb6777c97345d46b74c6cbf505c94cd2617a8d9c05fc3d0
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: This records the aviary analogy as an illustrative model for the possession/having distinction; it does not establish that the model is eventually accepted as adequate for explaining false judgment.
review_status: rejected
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0226
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 198d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There are two kinds of hunting: hunting before possession for the sake of acquiring, and hunting by one who already possesses, for the sake of taking hold of and having in hand what was long ago possessed; likewise one can re-learn and take hold of knowledges that one already possessed but did not have ready to hand in thought."
greek_terms:
  - θήρα
  - κτήσει
  - καταμανθάνειν
  - πρόχειρον
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 198d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids:
  []
limits: This records the two-hunting distinction proposed to address the puzzle of the arithmetician; it does not establish that the distinction fully resolves the difficulty of false judgment.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0227
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Since possessing knowledge and having knowledge have been distinguished, it is impossible for someone not to possess what he has acquired; thus it never happens that one does not know what one knows, though it is possible to acquire a false judgment about it.
greek_terms:
  - κεκτῆσθαι
  - ἔχειν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 199a

  []
limits: This records the resolution proposed for the "not knowing what one knows" puzzle; it does not establish that the account successfully handles all cases of false judgment, and Socrates will shortly identify a further difficulty.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0228

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: False judgment occurs when, in hunting for a knowledge among those fluttering about, one makes a mistake and takes hold of one knowledge in place of another — for example, taking the knowledge of eleven in place of the knowledge of twelve, like taking a ringdove instead of a pigeon.
greek_terms:
  - μεταλλαγὴ
  - φάτταν
  - περιστερᾶς
  - ἁμαρτὼν

observation_ids:

limits: This records the proposed mechanism for false judgment within the aviary model (mistaking one knowledge for another); it does not establish that this mechanism withstands scrutiny, since Socrates immediately raises a further difficulty about the very exchange of knowledges producing false judgment.
review_status: rejected
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0231
source_work: Theaetetus

speaker: ΘΕΑΙ.
claim_kind: definition
content: The aviary model should include pieces of ignorance flying alongside pieces of knowledge in the soul, so that the hunter sometimes grasps knowledge and sometimes ignorance, producing true opinion by knowledge and false opinion by ignorance.

  - ἀνεπιστημοσύνας
  - ἀνεπιστημοσύνην
  - ἐπιστήμην
stance_events:

```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0235

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: True opinion is not the same as knowledge, because a juror can be rightly persuaded about matters known only to an eyewitness and arrive at a true opinion without knowledge, yet a skilled juror could not judge correctly without knowledge if true opinion and knowledge were identical.
greek_terms:
  []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 201a-201c
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing

limits: This record does not establish that the jury argument is presented as a conclusive refutation; it is an argument that true opinion and knowledge appear to be different. The scope is limited to the specific counterexample of courtroom persuasion.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

      end_char: 138819
      text_sha256: fa53a2b479055ec74c7af51f485c3f6085e53505db7446f1090f43f7b89870e0
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0615
limits: Socrates applies the differentia-logos account to the case of knowing a person, setting up the examination that begins at the end of the segment. He then notes that his right opinion already grasps something common rather than the differentia, so the account is under tension but not yet refuted.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_theaetetus_0273
source_work: Theaetetus
stephanus_span: 209d-209e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
  stephanus_span: 209d-209e
  start_marker: 209d
  end_marker: 209e
  start_char: 139666
  end_char: 140479
  text_sha256: 573f4ee90537fa736e803c223f7413fc3e12bb9294e8579b160a68beefebee50
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: If 'adding an account to correct judgement' means forming an additional judgement about how something differs from other things, then the requirement is absurdly circular, since it demands that one add the very judgement one already has.
greek_terms:
  - προσλαβεῖν λόγον τῇ ὀρθῇ δόξῃ
  - προσδοξάσαι
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: 209d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 209d
      start_marker: 209d
      end_marker: 209d
      start_char: 139666
      end_char: 140069
      text_sha256: 5d28350d71bc589ed90e3b6382f62111fd1877848c0addc5a16591e1558aae37
  - kind: challenged
    stephanus_span: 209d-209e
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/theaetetus.txt
      stephanus_span: 209d-209e
      start_marker: 209d
      end_marker: 209e
      start_char: 139666
      end_char: 140479
      text_sha256: 573f4ee90537fa736e803c223f7413fc3e12bb9294e8579b160a68beefebee50
final_status: unresolved_challenge
observation_ids:
  - obs_theaetetus_0616
limits: This is a reductio of one interpretation of 'adding an account,' not a refutation of all possible interpretations. Socrates treats the interpretation as comic and describes it as fitting a blind man's instruction.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
```
